Present Spiritual Significance: 

None of the principles here enumerated can be ignored without weakening the force of others. There is no effective missionary work except as those converted become a highly moral people, practicing the law regarding temporalities and living at peace with each other. Similarly, there is no high morality among people who are blind and deaf to the claims of the poor. 19

Introduction: 

LESSON EIGHTEEN Things to Come 

(Sections 28: 2-8; 43: 5-7; 45: 2-10; 85: 24-35)

The early Saints had a strong feeling that God is working out his purposes in history, and that the major events of the future are pre-determined by the nature of God and the nature of man. They looked for a literal Zion, and they looked also for a literal fulfillment of the prophecies of judgment on the wicked. 

Major Emphases: 

The certainty of coming judgments.¹ 

The reunion of the righteous.3 millennial events.5 

The return of Christ in triumph.2 

The millennial reign.*

Post- 1. How do natural events illustrate the working of the law of cause and effect? What is the relation of coming events to our present way of living? 

In what way should the voice of these events call men to repentance? 

2. Under what conditions will the second coming take place? By whom will the Lord be accompanied? Who will rise from the dead at that time? 

3. What are the major indications that the coming of the Lord is near at hand? Is it important that we shall know the exact time of the second coming? Discuss this, briefly. 

4. What are the major outlines of the future destiny of the Jews? Are they reserved for this great destiny because of promises made to their fathers, or because of their fundamental character, or both? Discuss this, briefly. 

5. Where will the great reunion of the righteous take place? When? 

Where will they live? What will they do? 

6. What are the characteristics of the millennial reign? How will it be terminated? 

7. When will the second resurrection take place? Who will come forth at that time? What will be the condition of Satan at that time? 

8. What significance have these prophecies in relation to our present spiritual well being?

Present Spiritual Significance: 

Contemplation of coming judgments and the return of the Lord Jesus Christ, invested life with serious purpose from which the early Saints could never escape. While it is better to spend time building the kingdom than in speculating on the minutae of future events, the assurance of the working out of the divine purpose is of great value in impressing us with a sense of the dignity of our calling and the glory of divine service. 20

Introduction: 

LESSON NINETEEN The Spiritual Gift 

(Sections 46, 50)

As the membership of the church grew, and as the Saints were blessed with the gifts of the gospel, it became necessary to have some recognized modes of procedure and some fundamental tests of the spiritual quality of their experiences. This need is met in the two important revelations which we are to discuss. 

Major Emphases:

The elders are to conduct the meetings as they are led by the Holy Spirit (17:9; 46: 1). "Seek earnestly the best gifts, always remembering for what they are given" (46: 4). ... . . . "Many false spirits are abroad in the earth" (50: 1).... "That which doth not edify is not of God" (50: 6).

1. Who should conduct the meetings of the Saints? What general principle is to guide them in this work? Does the restriction of this task to the 

Melchisedec priesthood have any special significance? 

2. What spiritual gifts are enumerated in this revelation? Why is it important that the Saints shall "always retain in their minds the gifts given to the church"? 

3. Is it possible that our individual gifts are meagerly expressed when the body of the church is unhealthy? What bearing does this have on the problem of church unity? 

4. What basic tests are proposed for determining the validity of the spiritual gifts? Can you suggest other tests? 

5. What safeguards against imposition are promised to the elders? Enumerate some of the more obvious indications that a "gift" is not of God? 

6. For what reasons do some persons seek to exercise gifts which are not properly theirs? What is the major punishment against such a practice?¹ 

7. In what spirit are false manifestations to be rebuked? What dangers are inherent in a boastful attitude in this matter?2 

8. Are the gifts named in these revelations the only spiritual gifts? If not, mention other gifts which may be profitably enjoyed in the assemblies of the Saints?

Present Spiritual Significance: 

In relation to the spiritual gifts, as elsewhere, sound Christian living is fundamentally important. People of proved spiritual integrity and under the guidance of experienced ministry will grow in spiritual grace and power and discrimination, and so come to exercise a constantly larger measure of spiritual authority through the enjoyment of the spiritual gifts. 21

LESSON TWENTY Further Instructions Regarding Stewardship Introduction: 

(Sections 48, 51)

The principles of stewardship had already been clearly stated, but further instruction was needed as the Saints began to gather at Kirtland. These sections contain such instruction. 

Major Emphases: 

Earn and save for the kingdom's sake. . . . Stewardships are determined according to family needs. . . . The importance of organization, and of legal status. . . . The storehouse principle.

1. Review, briefly, the instructions regarding stewardship given prior to the date of these revelations.¹ 

2. Discuss, briefly, the importance of the instructions regarding industry and thrift: "Save all that you can." "Obtain all that you can in righteousness." What more recent statement of revelation is related to this instruction ?2 

3. Who was in charge of allocating stewardships? What four principles were to be followed in allocating stewardships? What is the reason for each of these? 

4. Who was directly concerned with the spiritual aspects of stewardship organization? Can the determination of equitable relationships between stewards be left to the operation of the spirit of good will, or is it desirable that stewards shall be organized on an agreed basis? Why? 

5. What was the purpose of giving the individual steward "a writing that shall secure unto him his portion"? What economic problem arises in a zionic community when a steward is found in transgression? How can both the steward and the church be protected under these circumstances? 

6. Did the organization proposed contemplate the private ownership of property? Did it contemplate group ownership? What safeguards are provided against the gains of successful group stewardships being dissipated. 

7. What is the purpose of the storehouse? Who is responsible for the administration of the storehouse for each stewardship group? From what source is this minister to be reimbursed? 

8. What spiritual qualities are vital to the effective functioning of the individual and group stewardships here outlined? 

Present Spiritual Significance: 

The emphasis in these revelations is on adequate organization effected through sharing the spirit of a great enterprise. Both of these elements are vital to stewardship success. 22

Introduction: 

LESSON TWENTY-ONE The Journey to Zion 

(Sections 52, 53, 54)

The first journey to Zion is a parable of our total church endeavor. The elders who had enlisted in the Cause of Zion went up to the promised land under divine direction, bearing testimony by the way. 

Major Emphases: 

The elders are to journey two by mony. . . Administer the ordinances sionary work.

two . . . Bear simple but fervent testi- . . The remainder should do local mis- 

1. When was the first missionary journey to Missouri undertaken? Who participated? 

2. What principle was followed in appointing the elders for this new missionary journey? How many missionary teams were sent out? 

3. What missionary preaching was done on the way? Why was such preaching peculiarly adapted to the circumstances? 

4. What specific instruction was given the elders regarding baptism?

How are they to know when the candidates were ready for baptism? 

5. What missionary task was given to the elders who remained near 

Kirtland? Discuss, briefly, the importance of local missionary endeavor. 

6. What group of Saints participated in the first mass movement to Independence? Who was their leader? What prior pioneering experience did these people have? 

7. For what reasons was it particularly desirable that the Saints should gather on the frontier? On what other frontiers did they subsequently gather? 

8. Discuss, briefly, the advantages gained from this missionary journey by those participating, by the church leaders who received their report, by the people who saw their counsel made significant through their example? 

Present Spiritual Significance: 

The simple missionary testimony of men whose own lives showed that they were in deadly earnest, could not fail to be effective. In our own day, such evident relation between our missionary message and the building of the kingdom will be similarly effective. 23

Introduction: 

LESSON TWENTY-TWO 

The Land of Zion 

(Section 57) 

The importance of actually designating a place for the gathering of the Saints cannot be overestimated. The journey to Zion was a great spiritual pilgrimage, and dedication of the promised land a great spiritual experience. 

Major Emphases: "This is the land of promise.' "Independence is the center place." "The land should be purchased by the Saints." . . . "Let those of whom

I have spoken be planted in the land of Zion." 

1. What prior intimations had been received regarding the location of the City of Zion? When was this location first definitely designated? What is the meaning of the term "the land of promise" in this connection? 

2. Describe, briefly, the dedication of the land of Zion. Why was the choice especially desirable? 

3. Who were present at the dedication of the place for the temple? Did the church own this place at this time? What was the area of the tract under discussion? With what means was the purchase made? Who purchased this land? 

4. State, briefly, the chain of title by which the Reorganized Church claimed the Temple Lot. State, also, the chain of title of Granville Hedrick and his associates and successors. 

5. What other land was to be purchased? What was the limit placed on the purchases? 

6. What principles were Sidney Gilbert to observe in his industrial concerns? For what purpose was he to conduct his store? What principles were to guide W. W. Phelps in his business affairs? 

7. What was the work of Bishop Partridge in Independence? Who were his associates? What training had they for this work? 

8. Discuss, briefly, the significance of this revelation. What major principles does it enunciate? What is its present importance?

Present Spiritual Significance: 

The designation of a literal place of gathering, where we must justify our spiritual purpose in terms of community life, forever commits the church to a practical demonstration of righteousness. 24

Introduction: 

LESSON TWENTY-THREE The Procedure in Gathering 

(Sections 53, 55, 56: 5-6, 57, 58) ex- In every important enterprise order and planned procedure are tremely important. In Zion building they are of paramount importance. It was difficult for the early Saints to realize this, and it had to be reiterated repeatedly throughout the revelations. 

Major Emphases: 

The order of the gathering.¹... "Let the work of the gathering be not in haste, nor by flight." "The sound must go forth from this place into all the world."

1. For what reason were the elders told to gather at Independence?? 

What disciplines are here imposed? What promises are made? 

2. In what order are the Saints to gather to Zion? Why must this order be observed? 

3. Who were instructed to locate in Independence at the beginning of the work of colonization? Why were these selected rather than others? 

4. What institutions were to be established in Independence? Does this indicate any reasons for gathering first the rich and learned, etc.? 

5. What is the purpose of the storehouse? Discuss, briefly, its place in a zionic community. 

6. What was the work of Edward Partridge in relation to the gathering? 

What instruction was given him regarding his work as a "judge in Israel"? 

7. What important counsel is here given the Saints regarding initiative and industry? Discuss this briefly. 

8. Why were the Saints advised that the gathering should not be in haste? 

Why were they required to gather under the direction of presiding officers? 

What procedure was recommended for the immediate future?3

Present Spiritual Significance: 

From the beginning the keynotes of the gathering have been urgency and preparation. They still are. We need to gather in order and under proper direction, and yet to be so keenly aware of the need for the gathering that we shall make every day a day of preparation for Zion no matter where we live. 25

Introduction: 

LESSON TWENTY-FOUR The Spiritual Standards of Zion (Sections 59, 63: 1-7; 64: 7) 

Zion building is essentially a spiritual enterprise. It was quite fitting, therefore, that the Saints should be reminded of the basic conditions of spiritual effectiveness as they began to gather in the center place. 

Major Emphases:

The importance of the moral law . . . The Lord's day . . . Thanksgiving 

Willing obedience.¹

1. What blessings are promised to those who gather to Zion? On what conditions are they promised? 

2. What great commandments were reiterated to the people of Zion? 

Discuss, briefly, the importance of the moral law. Discuss its social importance. 

3. Why is gratitude mentioned so prominently?? What is the relation between gratitude and effective worship? Between effective worship and righteousness? 

4. What instruction was given to the Saints in Zion regarding Sabbath keeping? What later instruction was given in this connection? Why is Sabbath keeping important? 

5. What is the meaning of the injunction "Confess thy sins unto thy brethren and before the Lord"? In what way does confession promote mental poise? What spiritual gains result from this practice? 

6. What is fasting? Is fasting compatible with partaking of food? What are the spiritual results of wastefulness? Why is extortion wicked? 

7. Who are the "wicked and rebellious" referred to?* Have they any modern counterparts? Are we justified in demanding "signs" as a condition of obeying the law? 

8. Discuss the law that "the Lord requireth the heart and a willing mind."5 

Enumerate the other spiritual standards which are fundamentally important. 

Present Spiritual Significance: 

Effective Zion building is dependent upon the spiritual quality of the people. Careful and willing observances of the moral law, the relaxation and recreation which comes from rest and genuine worship, the power which comes from complete dedication to a great cause, and the strength borne of willing compliance without bargaining, are all directly contributory to this effectiveness. 26

LESSON TWENTY-FIVE 

Building the Kingdom (Sections 60, 62, 63, 64, 68)

Following the dedication of the land of Zion a number of revelations were given in relation to missionary work, the gathering, and the mutual relationships of the ministry. The high points of these revelations are considered here. 

Major Emphases:

Missionary testimony¹. . . Land for Zion is to be secured by purchase² 

The importance of tithing³ . . . The work of the Bishopric¹ . . . The baptism of children.5

1. Re-state the instructions in the revelations given in Zion and on the return journey."

How were they organized for this ministry? What counsel was given them regarding the use of their talents?

8. What are the instructions given regarding the baptism of children? To what children does this particularly apply? Why? Under what responsibility are parents in this regard?

Present Spiritual Significance: 

There is no clear line of distinction between missionary work, local growth and Zion building. Principles which are basic to one. 

Principles which are basic to one are significant also in relation to the others. Such fundamentals as missionary testimony, the maintenance of peace, sharing the total burden through tithing and the religious education of children, are vitally important in every field of church activity. 27

LESSON TWENTY-SIX Stewardship Development 

(Sections 70, 72, 75: 4, 5; 77: 1, 2; 81, 82)

The problem of supporting those giving their full time to church work became quite acute as the church grew. So did the problems of organizing for stewardship and for the management of church concerns in Kirtland and The situation was accentuated by the friction between Independence and Kirtland. These revelations were of great value in meeting these difficulties. Zion.

The "stewards over the revelations."¹ The duty of the bishop in 

Kirtland. . . . The support of missionaries' families.² . . . The law on equality. . . . The status of women and children.5

2. In what sense are all stewards equal? What intrinsic rewards accompany the various types of stewardship?

What was the special purpose of the organization brought into being in response to Doctrine and Covenants 77? 

7. Who were the members of the first "United Order"? What was their purpose? Why were these persons included? What rights and responsibilities were committed to them?⁹ 

8. What is the status of widows under the law of stewardship? What is the status of minor children? What is the status of children who are of age? What is the status of orphans whose parents lived the stewardship law? 

Present Spiritual Significance: 

Effective organization for building the kingdom of God will include recognition of the duties and responsibilities and rights of those participating, and of their dependents. But this organization will only be made effective when the Saints are imbued with the spirit of sacrificial devotion to the Cause and think in terms of the Cause rather than in terms of their added security in the zionic community. 28

LESSON TWENTY-SEVEN The Destiny of Man (Section 76) 

The revelation on the glories is attested by the moral integrity of Joseph and Sidney Rigdon, by its harmony with other Scriptures, and by its intrinsic quality. The doctrines here enunciated are distinctive and beautiful. They will attract good men and women whenever they are intelligently presented. 

Major Emphases:

Additional testimony concerning the resurrection. The nature of celestial glory. . . . The nature of terrestrial glory. . . . The nature of telestial glory. . . . The sons of perdition.

1. What circumstances led to the receipt of this revelation? What evidences indicate its genuineness? 

2. Why is it impossible for God to honor a wicked man? Are the rewards of righteousness added from without or developed from within? 

3. What important testimony did Joseph and Sidney bear in this revelation? Why is it important? What is the meaning of "last of all” in this connection? 

4. Who are the sons of perdition? What is their destiny? What is the second death? 

5. For what purpose did Jesus come into the world? How is his work related to our salvation? How is it related to the resurrection of all men? 

6. What do we mean by the phrase "celestial glory"? "Terrestrial glory"? 

Telestial glory"? 

7. Who will participate in each of these glories? Who will participate in none of them? Who will come forth in the resurrection of the just? 

8. What specific task will be assigned to those who attain celestial glory? 

To those who attain the glory of the moon? In what way will life in the hereafter be related to the present earth life of men? 

Present Spiritual Significance: 

The conditions of his birth have a profound effect throughout every man's life, an effect which can never be fully eradicated. This is also true in the hereafter. Our status in Eternity is directly related to the quality of our lives here. 29

LESSON TWENTY-EIGHT 

Priesthood (Sections 42: 5, 83, 84, 104; 119: 2, 3, 9; 125: 14)

Introduction: 

Instruction regarding the work of the priesthood was given as needed. 

It is summarized in Doctrine and Covenants 83, 84, and 104, and will be studied in the next three lessons. 

Major Emphases:

The importance of ordination. . . . The conditions of effective ministry. 

Lineage and priesthood. . . . The duty of supporting the priesthood. 

1. What two priesthoods are there in the church? What are the duties of these priesthoods? What is the Levitical priesthood? 

2. What is the fundamental rule regarding ordinations?¹ Why must a branch approve ordinations? Why is ordination important? 

3. What fundamental conditions of effective ministry were named in early revelations ?3 Discuss, briefly, the further conditions enumerated later.¹ 

4. What are the major tasks of the priesthood? Discuss the importance of Doctrine and Covenants 42: 5. 

5. In what sense are the ministry to teach rather than to be taught?5 

What instruction is given to ministers regarding study?" In view of this and other Scriptures what is the significance of the instruction to "take no thought beforehand"?" 

6. What do we mean by lineal priesthood? Does this involve a promise of ordination because of ancestry, or a responsibility to prepare one's children for ministry? 

7. In what sense are the ministry "sanctified by the spirit unto the renewing of their bodies" ? To whom are the blessings of the ministry extended in addition to the ministers themselves? 

8. Under what conditions will the Lord respect the ministry of the church?10 Discuss this briefly.

Present Spiritual Significance: 

The body of the ministry share, as their joint stewardship, the whole life and future of the church. But the church as a whole is also responsible for providing an atmosphere and background in which men can develop and exercise their priesthood possibilities under the most favorable circumstances. It is only when there is creative interaction between priesthood and the body of the church that either the ministry or the people can be fully blessed. 30

Introduction: 

LESSON TWENTY-NINE The Word of Wisdom 

(Sections 49:3; 59: 4, 5; 86)

It is extremely important that we shall make a wise approach to the study of this revelation. Radical interpretation on the one hand, and unwillingness to accept discipline in matters of diet on the other hand, have both tended to blind the Saints to the remarkable values of this instruction. 

Major Emphases:

Protection against adulteration. . . . The prohibition against strong drinks and hot drinks. . . . The emphasis on herbs, fruits and grains. . . . The sparing use of meat.

1. Where was this revelation given? To whom was it given? Why is the introduction worded as it is? 

2. What prophetic anticipation of the adulteration of food is contained here? How fully has this been vindicated? 

3. What is the teaching of this section regarding "strong drinks"? What is the distinction between "strong drinks" and "hot drinks"? What is the teaching regarding the use of tobacco? 

4. What is meant by the phrase "every herb in the season thereof"? 

Is this a prohibition against the use of these foods out of season? 

5. What earlier teaching had the Saints received concerning the use of meat (49: 3; 59: 4)? What additional instruction is now given? Is the use of meat prohibited? 

6. Discuss, briefly, the connection between this revelation and scriptural instruction regarding temperance, adequate sleep, high moral standards, etc. 

7. What promises accompany observance of the Word of Wisdom? Do these promises apply to faithfulness to the commandment or to all commandments? Are we justified in making obedience to the Word of Wisdom a test of fellowship? 

8. What are the fundamental principles emphasized in this revelation?

Present Spiritual Significance: 

Obedience to the Word of Wisdom is important. Nevertheless, we are not justified in placing greater emphasis on obedience to the commandment concerning food than on the commandments having to do with other aspects of saintly living. Here, as elsewhere, the spirit of wisdom must guide. 31

LESSON THIRTY Education and the School of the Prophets 

(Sections 55: 2; 57: 5; 85: 21, 36, 46; 87: 5; 90: 12; 125: 16)

Introduction: 

From the first the Saints had an enlightened attitude towards education, and took the lead in establishing schools in the communities which they founded. 

Their educational activities extended to adults as well as to children, and special emphasis was laid on the importance of an educated ministry. 

Major Emphases:

Plans for school literature.¹ The school in Zion.² . . . The curriculum of the school of the prophets.³ Spiritual attitude in understanding.¹ 

1. What was the early church attitude toward the education of children? 

Name some of the leaders in adult education. 

2. What topics were studied in the school of the prophets. Is such study desirable for the preparation of the minister of today? 

3. What is the meaning of the statement "seek learning even by study and also by faith"? In what way was this illustrated in the school of the prophets? 

4. Discuss, briefly, the relation between effective organization, prayer, fasting, and the acquisition of understanding. 

5. What is the relation between the various admonitions in Doctrine and 

Covenants 86: 38 and the acquisition of knowledge? Who may participate in classes in the school of the prophets? Who may teach? 

6. What specific instruction was given to the Presidency concerning their study?5 Discuss the advantages accruing through observance of this counsel. 

What further instruction was later given to the Presidency regarding their studies? Why? 

7. What major institution of learning has been fostered by the church in more recent years? Has this been approved by revelation? 

8. What are the dangers of education? What are the safeguards of education? What is the place of education in the building of Zion? 

Present Spiritual Significance: 

Our history and our scriptures combine to indicate the importance of education, but attitude of heart is as important as keenness of mind. The instruction concerning the school of the prophets anticipates many of the progressive ideas of modern leaders in religious education. 32

Introduction: 

LESSON THIRTY-ONE 

Law and Achievement 

(Sections 85: 4-12; 90: 1-6) 

Many good people believe that the study of the word of God and good personal character together guarantee salvation. Under the guidance of 

Divinity Joseph taught the Saints that in any field of life success is only possible to those who learn and obey the laws operative in that field. This is especially emphasized in the revelation now under review. 

Major Emphases:

The earth will be crowned with glory in fulfilling the purpose of its creation.¹... "That which is governed by law, is also preserved by law."2 "Unto every kingdom is given a law."³ The law of the celestial kingdom is the law of light and life and growth.*

1. What is the greatest glory which can come to any created thing? For what purpose was the earth created? When will it serve this purpose? "'5 2. Discuss the meaning of the statement that "they who are not sanctified through the law which I have given you, must inherit another kingdom.' 

Why is this so? 

3. Discuss, briefly, "Men of celestial quality inherit a world of celestial opportunity." Is this true now? Is it true against the background of eternity? 

4. Illustrate the fact that "that which is governed by law is also preserved by law." 

5. Is it true that not even the mercy of God can change the operation of law, but only cause us to know and love the law? Does the reign of law hamper or promote our freedom? 

6. What is intelligence? What is the source of intelligence? How is intelligence related to agency? In what way do truth and intelligence minister to celestial glory? 

7. In what sense is it true that "light and truth forsake the evil one"? 

Illustrate your answer. 

8. How may we learn the purpose of God for us? How may we secure the values of celestial life?

Present Spiritual Significance: 

The vast spiritual riches which the church has accumulated during the years, are available to teach us the celestial law and to persuade us to put this law into operation in our lives. We shall only become truly free as we learn more of the demands of freedom and obey them with eager devotion. 33

Introduction: 

LESSON THIRTY-TWO Renounce War and Proclaim Peace 

(Section 95) From the beginning of the settlement in Missouri there was friction between the Saints and their "Gentile" neighbors. This culminated in the expulsion of the Saints from Jackson County. It was against this general background, although some of these events had not yet transpired, that Joseph received the revelation dated August 15, 1833.¹ 

Major Emphases: 

The ultimate glory of the righteous.2 

Freedom comes through obedience to law. . . . Renounce war and proclaim peace. . . . The law on retaliation.3

1. Outline briefly the situation in the church in August, 1833. 

2. Discuss, briefly, "all things work together for good to them that love 

God." Do you agree? 

3. What instruction was received by the Saints regarding their support of the civil law? Why is obedience to law especially important in times of crisis? 

4. Do you agree that "it is useless to change one form of government for another unless the better form of government is administered by better men?" 

Consider this in connection with the statement "when the wicked rule the people mourn" ?5 

5. Note the conditions under which the Saints were told to "renounce war and proclaim peace." Is this counsel practicable in our personal concerns today? 

In our economic affairs? In national affairs? 

6. Do you agree that "nothing their enemies could do could hurt them so much as their own grudging spirit in regard to the things of the kingdom"? Consider, briefly, which can harm us most, enemies without or enemies within. 

7. Outline the instruction on self-restraint and forgiveness in Doctrine and Covenants 93: 5-7. Discuss the application of this procedure in modern life. Can it be successfully applied by persons who have themselves been offenders? 

8. What should be the Latter Day Saint attitude toward participation in the duties of citizenship? Toward the promotion of industrial peace? Toward the promotion of international peace?

Present Spiritual Significance: 

We are not committed to a program of peace because it is the easy way, but because it is the best way. Part of the task of building the kingdom lies in the establishment of institutions and procedures which will promote justice and therefore eliminate the causes of friction. 34

LESSON THIRTY-THREE Zion Shall Not Be Moved (Sections 98, 102: 7) 

When word was received of the expulsion of the Saints from Jackson County, the Saints at Kirtland were astounded. They needed both explanation and reassurance regarding the ultimate success of the zionic enterprise. 

These were given in the revelation of December 16, 1833. 

Major Emphases:

The reason for the expulsion. . . . Zion shall not be moved out of her place. . The parable of Zion. Continue to gather. 

1. To what degree were the Saints responsible for the difficulties in 

Missouri?1 What more recent emphasis has been placed on the importance of peaceable and friendly settlement ?2 

2. Suggest some typical reactions among the Saints when they received news of the expulsion. What definite instruction was given regarding the place of gathering? 

3. What promises were made in connection with the return of the Saints to Zion? Who were to return? Is there any indication here of the long time to elapse before the return? 

4. What is the meaning of the the holy places of our church history. 

5. What promises were made to the Saints for the more distant future? 

What effect would these promises have on their courage in face of calamity? term "holy places"? Name several of 

How is a place made holy? 

6. Tell, in brief, the parable recounted in this revelation. What aspects of this prophetic parable point to the work of the Reorganization? 

7. What instructions were given the Saints regarding the further gathering to Missouri? To what place in Missouri did the gathering continue? What area was included in "the regions roundabout"? 

8. Discuss, briefly, the important contribution made to the spiritual growth of the Saints by the instruction to seek redress through legal procedure.3 

Present Spiritual Significance: 

We are frequently tempted to think that the difficulties inherent in building the kingdom of God come from without. Actually, the major difficulties arise from our own lack of zionic insight, devotion and ability. When we match our great calling with a great character, and this great character, with highly developed talent and insight, the way will yet be opened before us. 35

LESSON THIRTY-FOUR 

Zion's Camp (Sections 100, 102)

The continued difficulties of the Saints in Missouri caused them to appeal to Joseph Smith and the other church leaders at Kirtland for aid. These attempted to gather men, money and provisions. Lack of response on the part of the eastern Saints and other difficulties made the enterprise unsuccessful. 

Major Emphases: of the camp. 

The purpose "Were it not for transgression, they might have been redeemed.”¹ "Let my army become very great, and let it be sanctified before me." "2 "Lift up an ensign of peace."

"3 1. Outline, briefly, the conditions leading to the call for Zion's camp. 

2. Under what conditions were the Saints promised victory? Does their failure mean that they were all unworthy? What is the significance of this fact?

4. Who was to go up to Zion? What equipment were they to take? Why? 

5. What are the indications concerning the peaceful nature of this enterprise? What procedure was planned? 

6. Enumerate some of the major transgressions of the Saints. What is their counterpart today? In this connection consider the statement, "Where your treasure is, there will your heart be also." 

7. Why did Zion's Camp fail to achieve its purpose? What secondary gains resulted from the enterprise? 

8. What important work was later done by members of Zion's Camp? 

What part may their experiences in the camp have had in preparing them for this work?

Present Spiritual Significance: 

The Saints are necessarily dependent on each other. The rewards which would properly accrue to men of real zionic quality are delayed until they have associates of equal quality. Zion cannot be built by good individuals, but only by a great people. 36

LESSON THIRTY-FIVE 

The Standing High Council (Sections 99; 104: 14, 15; 122: 6; 129: 7)

The Bishop is appointed as a judge over matters involving the Saints.¹ 

It soon became apparent in the church that there was need for a court to which appeals could be taken from the Bishops' Court. Accordingly councils of twelve High Priests were called on a few occasions to consider matters of importance. This was an evident preliminary to the formal organization of the High Council as noted in this section. 

Major Emphases:

If possible settle personal difficulties without court action.2 Offenses against the civil law should be dealt with by civil authorities.³ ... Determination of the guilt or innocence of the accused is reserved to the judicial arm. 

There is no appeal from the decision of the High Council. . . . The advisory functions of the High Council.

1. What is the first step in dealing with cases of difficulty within the church? Who should take the initiative? At what point should members of the priesthood be brought into the endeavor toward reconciliation? 

2. What courts are provided for in the church law? Which of these are temporary and which are permanent? What church official is described as a "Judge in Israel"? 

3. Enumerate the types of High Councils contemplated in the law. What is the function of each? Who presides over each? 

4. When was the standing High Council of the church first organized? 

How was the work of the council done prior to that time? 

5. How are members of the High Council appointed? How are emergency members of the council selected? 

6. Who may preside over the High Council? What recourse does he have in situations where the law is not adequate or clearly understood? 

7. Why are cases involving general officers tried before the High Council rather than before local courts? What is the usual procedure in safeguarding the interests of the accused in the High Council?. 

8. What responsibility does the High Council have in relation to the administration of church funds? What is their relation to the Bishopric in this connection?

Present Spiritual Significance: 

The standing high council should be available for constantly more important work in connection with the establishment of Zion, and should therefore be relieved from the burden of petty cases which previously demanded attention. This may be achieved in two ways: by the codification of church law and by the growth of understanding and harmony among the Saints. 37

LESSON THIRTY-SIX 

Stewardship Readjustment (Section 101) Earlier revelation had instructed the Saints to organize in the "United Order" under which properties were held in Independence and Kirtland under common direction. The difficulties in Missouri made it impossible to continue this arrangement, and the Order was accordingly dissolved. The basic purposes of the Order were maintained through individual and group stewardships. 

Major Emphases: १९ The prior history of the Order.... The wickedness of breaking the covenant.2 Appoint every man his stewardship." . . . Local organization of stewards.* . . . “Pay all "Pay all your debts."5

1. Relate, briefly, the prior history of the "United Order of Enoch.” Why was it now necessary to dissolve this Órder? 

2. What internal difficulties militated against the success of the United 

Order? What light does this throw on the importance of spiritual factors in building the kingdom? 

3. Does a man have a legal right to withdraw from a stewardship enterprise? Does he have a moral right? 

4. Enumerate some of the decisions of life which ought to be irrevocable, and upon which other persons venture their lives. Is the stewardship covenant intended to involve such a decision? Discuss this briefly. 

5. Why were some of us born with good health and social, financial, educational and spiritual opportunities, while others are born in backward lands and face life with heavy handicaps from the start? Consider this in the light of Doctrine and Covenants 101: 2. 

6. What is the explanation given by Joseph Smith III for the names used in this revelation? Is it reasonable to suppose that the fundamental principles of stewardship are unchanging? Why? 

7. Discuss, briefly, the place of the common treasury in the new order. 

What values are conserved through the operation of the storehouse principle? 

8. What costly enterprise was being carried forward by the Saints at this time? What was the instruction given the Saints regarding their debts? What later instruction was given in the same connection?" 

Present Spiritual Significance: 

Responsibility in the kingdom of God should not be lightly undertaken, but once undertaken it should be held sacred. Brotherhood is an indissoluble relationship. He who withdraws from the brotherhood because his individual interests are not fully conserved is not worthy of membership in the kingdom. 38

LESSON THIRTY-SEVEN The Melchisedec Priesthood 

(Sections 83, 84, 104)

The instruction regarding the work of the Melchisedec priesthood gradually laid the foundation for the official structure of the church. It is still fundamentally important, for there is no church membership without the ministry of the Melchisedec priesthood.

The power and authority of the Melchisedec priesthood. . The functions of the Melchisedec priesthood. .. The place of the quorums.

. . The importance of organization.

3. What is the relation between the high priesthood and the Melchisedec priesthood? What are the duties of the High Priest?2 Through what group of priesthood do the presidency of the church preside over the church? Into what five major sections is all the work of the church divided? What high priests direct these divisions of the total task?

6. What is the primary work of the High Council? What is the distinction between the traveling High Council and the standing High Council? 

7. What is the work of the Evangelical Minister? By whom are evangelists called? In what sense is the evangelist a "prophet and high priest”? 

8. What are the duties of the bishop? What dual priesthood does the bishop hold? In what sense is the bishop "a judge in Israel"?

Present Spiritual Significance: 

The brethren of the Melchisedec priesthood are but men, and make many mistakes. They are nevertheless called with a holy and significant calling, and if their preparation is matched by the support and expectancy of the body of the Saints, their spiritual guidance will lead to the building of the kingdom. 39

LESSON THIRTY-EIGHT 

The Aaronic Priesthood (Sections 17: 10-12; 42: 5; 83: 3-6; 104: 10, 32, 38-40)

Introduction: 

The progress of the church depends upon the effective functioning of the standing ministry, among whom the brethren holding the Aaronic priesthood have an important place. The privileges and duties of this ministry are repeatedly emphasized in the revelations. 

Major Emphases:

The authority of the Aaronic priesthood. . . The duties of this ministry 

The organization of the Aaronic priesthood.

3. What are the other grades of the Aaronic priesthood? What are the duties of each office of this priesthood?

Introduction: 

LESSON FIFTY 

Revelation and Experience

Looking backward over our course of study we can see that revelation involves three elements. A loving Father, needy children, and capacity on the part of these children to understand that which the Father wishes to communicate. The possibility of divine revelation is therefore dependent on our capacity as individuals and as groups. As we grow more like God, so He can reveal Himself to us more fully. This is true of the church, as well as of individuals. 

1. List the factors in divine revelation. Which are constant and which are variable? What obligation does this put on us? 

2. Do you agree that the possibility of divine revelation is limited by our lack of capacity. Can a good man know as much about God and His purposes as a cleverer wicked man?

The Revelations Are Related to Definite Historical Situations: 

The revelations in the book of Doctrine and Covenants were received by men and by the church in specific situations as a means of increasing their understanding, guiding their actions and deepening their devotion. 

If we are to understand these revelations they must be studied in relation to the circumstances under which they were received, for they were not isolated pronouncements but part of the warp and woof of our church history. 

3. Do you agree that the word of God is communicated to men in connection with specific situations? What does this indicate regarding further revelation? 

4. In order to emphasize the importance of history in understanding revelation, discuss the value of a history of the church written in Biblical style and including each revelation in the Doctrine and Covenants in its appropriate place and in some such form as "And the Lord said unto Joseph. .

The Revelations Are Cumulative: 

Revelation Our education proceeds from the known to the unknown. proceeds similarly. When properly understood and incorporated into the life of the church, earlier instructions, including the earlier Scriptures, become the basis of further instruction. Principles revealed in earlier days are thus elaborated as the experience of the individual and of the church become more mature. There are no contradictions in the revelations but, instead, there is a definite line of development. 

5. All subsequent education depends on the clearness of our understanding of fundamental principles. Discuss this in relation to growth in the knowledge of God. 

6. Do you agree that there is a definite line of development running through the revelations? Illustrate this from the revelations of the late Joseph 

Smith in regard to church organization. 52

Present Spiritual Significance: 

The revelation of God is still growing in the lives of good men and in the life of the church. It consists in experience rather than in a written record, although the written record preserves the memory of the original experience and is a means of further inspiration under the illumination of the Spirit of God. It is not possible to scale the heights of spiritual understanding at one bound, but with eternity before us, and the assurance of the unfailing love of God, those who put their trust in Him can grow from grace to grace. 

7. Has God revealed Himself to you in your growing experience? Do you agree that he is creating a growing "tradition of the elders" which embodies our common understanding of His mind and will up to our present point of development? Why is this important? 

8. Discuss the relation between revelation and life. What is the meaning of the statement of Jesus, "This is life eternal, that they might know Thee the only true God and Jesus Christ whom Thou hast sent.' "'1 53

LESSON FIFTY-ONE The Prophetic Element in the Doctrine and Covenants Introduction: 

In the long run, the Doctrine and Covenants will stand or fall according to its intrinsic spiritual authority. Its full and rich use in the church, and its power to win men to God, await the time when its claims can be critically examined and its quality can bring it vindication. 

1. In what different senses is the book of Doctrine and Covenants authoritative? Which of these is most fundamental and enduring? 

2. Will the Doctrine and Covenants commend itself best on its own merits, or when its teachings are embodied in the life of the church? Discuss this briefly.

Prophets and Prophecy: 

The prophets of Israel were men of rich spiritual genius, alert to current events, but interpreting them in the light of their own close communion with divinity. They had the habit of probing into the basic moralities involved in every situation, and were capable of deep and sustained indignation in the presence of wickedness and pretended piety. 

They were the foretellers of future events, and many of their manifestations were notably fulfilled. Their powers to foretell the future arose in large measure because of their assurance of the character of God. Yet their chief function was not to foretell the future, but to reveal God to the men of their day.

The essence of the prophetic function is: Seeing God clearly, revealing the nature of God, interpreting the demands of God in the lives of men, foretelling the future. 

3. When you know a man well, can you foretell what he will do under certain circumstances? Is this true of God? In what is it true that every man who has "the testimony of Jesus" has also "the spirit of prophecy"? 

4. Were the prophets chiefly interested in foretelling the details of future history, or in shaping the lives of the men of their own generation? Discuss this briefly. 

Foretelling and Forthtelling:

Prophecies which seek to foretell the future have a powerful attraction for many, but this is creative only when it is related to the facts of the present. 

Failure to realize this has limited the value of prophecy in the lives of many people.

Part of the function of the prophets is that of setting forth the nature of God so clearly that men realize the divine holiness and respond to the demands which God has laid on them by his very goodness. 

Jesus was the greatest of the prophets. Like them he was conscious of the majesty of divinity, of his divine commission to bring men to God, and of the wide disparity between the divine purpose for men and the actual lives 54

of men. 

Like them, he possessed capacity for sustained moral indignation. 

Like them, he foretold the future. But more than all of them he revealed the nature and purpose of God, who controls the future. 

5. What is the importance of prophetic foretelling? What is the importance of prophetic forthtelling? What is the relation between the two? 

6. In what sense is Jesus the greatest of the prophets?

The Prophetic Character of the Doctrine and Covenants: 

The spiritual stature of a prophet is chiefly measured by the clarity and adequacy of his revelation of the mind and will of God. Measured by this standard, the revelations contained in the Doctrine and Covenants indicate that the prophets of this dispensation have been men of genuine spiritual quality. 

There are nevertheless differing grades of prophetic values, even in the Doctrine and Covenants. A revelation indicating that a certain man shall occupy in a certain place is important, but is not nearly so important against the background of eternity as a further disclosure of the nature of God, or a further definition of the character and purpose of his kingdom. These may at the time seem too prosaic to receive the consideration of godly men, and yet they may embody the greatest revelation of divinity to that age. 

The revelations in the book of Doctrine and Covenants are prophetic in their revelation of the nature of God, in their disclosure of the nature of the kingdom, in their emphasis on fundamental spiritual values, in their foretelling of the future. 

7. What is the measure of the spiritual stature of a prophet? In your opinion, what are the most distinctively prophetic revelations in the Doctrine and Covenants? Why? 

8. In what various ways does the book of Doctrine and Covenants justify its claim to be regarded as Scripture? 55

Introduction: 

LESSON FIFTY-TWO The Revelations Which Are to Come 

No man whose life is disordered by conflicting purposes is likely to receive a full and rich revelation of the mind and will of God. This is true of the church, also. Yet as we grow in unity and become more truly the children of God we may rightfully expect progressive unveiling of divinity. 

1. Discuss, briefly, the relation between church unity and divine revelation. Is the unity needed a unity of understanding, or a unity of life on a high plane of spiritual quality? 

2. Do you agree that the growth of the church can be measured by the degree in which the principles of past revelation become the guiding principles of our individual and church lives? Will we need further revelation in order to make this possible?

What Further Revelation May We Anticipate? 

We shall not need much further revelation to enable us to stand still, but shall need constant guidance in order to build the kingdom of God. Naturally, we shall need light regarding changes of personnel in the priesthood, and especially in the presiding quorums. But if we are to give moral and spiritual leadership to our generation we shall also need divine guidance on such problems as:

The social and economic structure of the kingdom of God. 

Missionary organization and emphasis in a changing world. 

The principles of effective ministry. 

The functions and functioning of auxiliary organizations. 

Enrichment of the social and spiritual standards of the family. 

Clarification of the place and principles of religious education. 

Relations with other people. 

3. Why do we not already have clear and final guidance in the fields here named? 

4. Discuss, briefly, our need of guidance along the lines mentioned. In what additional fields do we need guidance? 

5. In what way will guidance along the lines indicated reveal God to us? 

Discuss, briefly, the statement that "all the work of God is a revelation of Him."

The Conditions of Divine Revelation: 

Light will come to us on such vital problems as these only as we are progressively obedient to that which has already been received and so develop a more earnest concern for the salvation of men and the building of the kingdom of God. Our Heavenly Father will bless us as we create the need for further light by pressing eagerly forward toward the frontiers of our pres- 56

ent illumination. Then, out of our expectancy, our devotion, our support of those whom He has called, and our growing capacity to understand, will come the guidance which shall lead us into the kingdom and thus into the presence of God. 

6. What are the basic conditions of divine revelation? importance. 

Discuss their 7. Is it possible for God to reveal Himself to us if we fail to abide these conditions? Why? 

8. Will the greatest revelation of the future be recorded in a book or in the lives of the people? Where is the most effective record now? What is the relation between the two?

Summary: 

The greatest gain to be derived from a study of the revelations is that change in our individual lives and in the life of the church which will enable our Heavenly Father to reveal Himself to us in constantly greater fullness. 

This revelation will not come to us in word only, but in the power of enlightened experience. God will reveal Himself in us as well as to us. 

Revelations to the church will become vocal through the prophet of the Lord; but they will be made possible by the intelligent and devoted living of a righteous people. It is our present task to use creatively the light already available, so as to merit the further endowment appropriate to our time and opportunity. 57

LEADERSHIP TRAINING 

The Commentary on the Doctrine and Covenants is an accredited course in the Leadership Training Curriculum Plan. It is Course No. 363. To get credit you may either: 

1. Write the answer to any five questions in each lesson, or 

2. Take four examinations and write four themes; one for each quarter of the course. 

3. A local class may be conducted with an approved or accredited teacher by completing all written assignments satisfactorily. 

Requirements for Credit

Each pupil must register with the local class or direct with the Department of Religious Education. The fee is twenty-five cents per quarter or one dollar for the course. He must attend at least ten sessions in each quarter. (Exceptions can only be made by the Department.) 

Requirements for the Teacher

An accredited teacher is one who has satisfactorily completed the course and received a certificate of credit.

An approved teacher is one who has not taken the course but has been approved by the Department upon a recommendation by the pastor or church school director of the branch.

The teacher is expected to grade the papers of the class and at the completion of the course report the final grades to the Department. 

He should write the department for further instructions. 

Requirements for Correspondence Courses 

Isolated members may register with the Department by paying a fee of fifty cents per quarter or two dollars for the course.

They must complete all written assignments and send them to the Department for grading.

Materials and Credits 

Those who satisfactorily complete the entire course will receive one standard unit of credit for each quarter. Order all supplies from the Herald
